# DOCUMENT 60: STATUTORY CLARIFICATION ON MEDICAL DEVICES PRICING, NPPA REVISIONS & RULE 26(C) / RULE 6(1)(E) HARMONIZATION (I-10/17/2023-W&M)
# ==============================================================================

## 1. Statutory Document Metadata

| Attribute | Statutory Detail |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Document Index** | Document 60 |
| **Source File** | `dataset/2023.7.10 Medical Devices revision of prices_1732709214.pdf` (1 page) |
| **Document Type** | Official Statutory Circular / Clarification |
| **Statutory Reference Number** | File No. `I-10/17/2023-W&M` |
| **Issuing Authority** | Government of India, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food & Public Distribution, Department of Consumer Affairs, Legal Metrology Division |
| **Issuing Location** | Krishi Bhawan, New Delhi-01 |
| **Date of Issuance** | 10.7.2023 (10th July, 2023) |
| **Target Recipients** | The Controller of Legal Metrology, All States / Union Territories (UTs) |
| **Authorized Signatory** | Ashutosh Agarwal, Deputy Director (Legal Metrology) |
| **Signatory Contact** | Phone: 011-23389489 | Email: `ashutosh.agarwal13@nic.in` |
| **Subject Heading** | Provisions of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 on Medical Devices -reg. |
| **Key Statutory Provisions Harmonized** | 1. Rule 26(c) Proviso of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011.<br>2. Rule 6(1)(e) Second Proviso of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011.<br>3. Drugs (Price Control) Order, 2013 (DPCO 2013) made under section 3 of the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 (10 of 1955).<br>4. National Pharmaceutical Pricing Authority (NPPA) Statutory Price Revision Notifications. |

---

## 2. Executive Summary & Metrological Enforcement Architecture

### A. The Intersection of Medical Devices, DPCO, and Legal Metrology
Prior to this clarification, ambiguity existed regarding the legal treatment of medical devices:
1. **Rule 26 Exemption Framework:** Rule 26(c) exempts scheduled and non-scheduled drug formulations covered under the *Drugs (Price Control) Order, 2013 (DPCO)* from Chapter II of the Packaged Commodities Rules.
2. **The Medical Device Carve-In (Rule 26(c) Proviso):** The proviso explicitly states:
   > *"Provided that no exemption shall be applicable to medical devices declared as drugs."*
   Thus, medical devices (e.g., blood pressure monitors, clinical thermometers, nebulizers, stents, surgical consumables) remain **fully subject** to the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011.

### B. Price Revisions by NPPA / Competent Authorities
Under the *Essential Commodities Act, 1955*, the National Pharmaceutical Pricing Authority (NPPA) periodically revises or fixes ceiling prices and retail prices of notified medical devices (e.g., linked to annual Wholesale Price Index adjustments or extraordinary price regulation):
1. **Rule 6(1)(e) Second Proviso Protection:** Rule 6(1)(e) mandates the declaration of MRP inclusive of all taxes, but its second proviso dictates:
   > *"Provided further that if the retail sale price of any essential commodity is fixed and notified by the Competent Authority under the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 the same shall apply."*
2. **Statutory Directive on Price Revision:** The Department of Consumer Affairs directs all State Controllers to instruct field officers that:
   - Where prices are permitted to be revised by the Government, NPPA, or any other statutory body under the Essential Commodities Act, medical device manufacturers and importers **must be permitted to revise prices accordingly**.
   - Field officers must not treat NPPA-authorized price revisions as tampering, re-stickering violations, or dual-pricing offences under Legal Metrology law.

### C. Direct Software Architecture Impact for SIH Problem Statement 26035
- **Dual-Regulatory Harmonization Engine:** When checking a medical device product:
  1. *Rule 26(c) Proviso Check:* Ensure full PCR declarations are present (medical devices do NOT get the pharmaceutical exemption).
  2. *Rule 6(1)(e) Proviso Check:* Connect with NPPA price ceiling databases to verify that the declared MRP does not exceed the statutory ceiling price fixed under DPCO / Essential Commodities Act, 1955.

---

## 3. Complete Verbatim Statutory Text

*(Official Statutory Circular issued by the Legal Metrology Division, Department of Consumer Affairs, Government of India)*

```text
                                    I-10/17/2023-W&M
                                  Government of India
               Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food &Public Distribution
                           Department of Consumer Affairs
                              Legal Metrology Division

                                                        Krishi Bhawan, New Delhi-01
                                                                       Dated: 10.7.2023

To,
        The Controller of Legal Metrology,
        All States / UTs

Subject: Provisions of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 on Medical
Devices -reg.

Sir,

        The undersigned is directed to refer to the above mentioned subject and to state that
Rule 26 of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 provides exemption from
the Rules, that:

"26. Exemption in respect of certain packages.-Nothing contained in these rules shall apply
to any package containing a commodity if- ...

(c) it contains scheduled formulations and non-scheduled formulations covered under the Drugs
(Price Control) Order, 2013 made under section 3 of the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 (10 of
1955):

        Provided that no exemption shall be applicable to medical devices declared as drugs."

2.      Rule 6(1)(e) of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 provides that:

"(e) the retail sale price of the package, shall clearly indicate that it is the maximum retail price
inclusive of all taxes in Indian currency:

Provided that for packages containing alcoholic beverages or spirituous liquor, the State Excise
Laws and the rules made there under shall be applicable within the State in which it is
manufactured and where the state excise laws and rules made there under do not provide for
declaration of retail sale price, the provisions of these rules shall apply.

Provided further that if the retail sale price of any essential commodity is fixed and
notified by the Competent Authority under the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 the
same shall apply."

3.      Therefore, it is requested to issue suitable directions to your field Officers to ensure the
compliance of the provisions of Rules. If the prices are allowed to be revised by the
Government/ NPPA/ any other Statutory Body, then the industries may be allowed to revise the
prices as per the Notification issued by Government/ NPPA/ any other Statutory Body.
```

# DOCUMENT 61: STANDARD OPERATING PROCEDURE (SOP) FOR DETERMINATION OF NET QUANTITY OF EDIBLE OILS & FATS IN PACKAGES (I-10/27/2021-W&M)
# ==============================================================================

## 1. Statutory Document Metadata

| Attribute | Statutory Detail |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Document Index** | Document 61 |
| **Source File** | `dataset/2023_12_29_Standard_Operating_Procedure_for_Edible_oil_&_Fats_Net.pdf` (5 pages) |
| **Document Type** | Official Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) & Enforcement Guidelines |
| **Statutory Reference Number** | File No. `I-10/27/2021-W&M` |
| **Issuing Authority** | Legal Metrology Division, Department of Consumer Affairs, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution, Government of India |
| **Issuing Location** | Krishi Bhawan, New Delhi-110001 |
| **Date of Approval / Signing** | 29.12.2023 (29th December, 2023) |
| **SOP Header Issue Date** | 15.01.2023 \| Rev. No.: 00 |
| **Authorized Signatory** | Ashutosh Agarwal, Deputy Director & I/c Director (Legal Metrology) |
| **Signatory Contact** | Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution, Krishi Bhawan, New Delhi-110001 |
| **Scope of Application** | Inspection and sampling at manufacturing plants, in-transit, Carrying & Forwarding Agents (CFAs), and retail markets across all States/UTs. |
| **Governing Enactments** | The Legal Metrology Act, 2009 and The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 (Fifth, Sixth, and Seventh Schedules). |

---

## 2. Executive Summary & Metrological Enforcement Architecture

### A. Background & The Volumetric vs. Gravimetric Challenge in Edible Oils
Edible oils and fats expand and contract significantly with temperature variations:
1. **The Enforcement Dilemma:** Packing edible oil by volume (e.g., 1 Litre) led to widespread consumer short-delivery complaints during winter (due to volumetric shrinkage) and deceptive practices where packaging temperatures altered delivered mass.
2. **Statutory Shift to Gravimetric Equivalency:** The Department of Consumer Affairs mandated that **all volumetric packs of edible oils and fats must be verified strictly by equivalent weight (gravimetrically)**, utilizing standardized temperature-referenced specific gravity conversion factors.

### B. Standard Specific Gravity Conversion Matrix (Paragraph 4)
The SOP establishes non-negotiable statutory density conversion benchmarks:
1. **Soft Oils (Mustard, Sunflower, Soybean, Groundnut, Sesame, etc.):**
   - **$910	ext{ g}$ for $1	ext{ Litre}$ at $30^\circ	ext{C}$** (Statutory density $
ho = 0.910	ext{ g/cm}^3$).
2. **Oils Other than Soft Oils (Palm-based oils, Palmolein, Vanaspati, Hydrogenated fats):**
   - **$893	ext{ g}$ for $1	ext{ Litre}$ at $50^\circ	ext{C}$** (Statutory density $
ho = 0.893	ext{ g/cm}^3$).
3. **Pure Ghee:**
   - **$900	ext{ g}$ for $1	ext{ Litre}$ at $45^\circ	ext{C}$** (Statutory density $
ho = 0.900	ext{ g/cm}^3$).

### C. Mandatory Unit Sale Price (USP) in Weight (Paragraph 5)
- Under Rule 6 of the *Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011*, the Unit Sale Price for edible oils and fats **must mandatorily be declared in WEIGHT** (i.e., per gram or per kilogram).
- Declaration of USP in volume (per ml or per litre) is strictly **optional / additional**, ensuring consumers can make uniform price-per-kilogram comparisons regardless of packaging temperature.

### D. The Two-Tier Tare Determination Protocol (Paragraph 3)
1. **Single-Package Tare Screening [Sub-para (1) & (2)]:**
   - If tare weight of 1 sample package $\le rac{3}{10}	imes MPE$ ($0.3	imes MPE$), this tare weight is legally presumed valid across all packages in the sample.
2. **Five-Package Tare Averaging [Sub-para (1) & (3)]:**
   - If initial tare weight $> 0.3	imes MPE$, four additional tare packages are measured (total 5).
   - If the variance $(	ext{Tare}_{\max} - 	ext{Tare}_{\min}) \le rac{4}{10}	imes MPE$ ($0.4	imes MPE$), the tare weight is established as the **arithmetic mean** of the 5 tare weights.
3. **Fifth Schedule Destructive / In-Line Sampling [Sub-paras (4) & (5)]:**
   - If tare variation $> 0.4	imes MPE$, empty packages must be selected across the lot according to the Fifth Schedule sampling matrix, marked, weighed, introduced into the automated filling line, and re-weighed after sealing.

### E. Direct Software Architecture Impact for SIH Problem Statement 26035
- **Automated Density & Equivalent Net Content Calculator:** NAWI and packaging verification algorithms must embed the statutory temperature-density table:
  $$	ext{Equivalent Mass (g)} = 	ext{Declared Volume (L)} 	imes 	ext{Conversion Factor (g/L)}$$
  - E.g., for a 5-Litre mustard oil jar: $	ext{Required Mass} = 5 	imes 910 = 4,550	ext{ g}$.
- **SOP Tare Validation Engine:** Automates the $0.3	imes MPE$ single-tare test and $0.4	imes MPE$ five-tare spread test before calculating net package weights in Seventh Schedule Form A.

---

## 3. Complete Verbatim Statutory Text

*(Standard Operating Procedure issued by the Legal Metrology Division, Department of Consumer Affairs, Government of India)*

```text
                                        I-10/27/2021-W&M        dt. 29.12.2023

+---------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                       | Determination of the Net Quantity of Commodities (Edible    |
|                                       | Oils & Fats) contained in any Package                       |
| Legal Metrology,                      +--------------------------+----------------------------------+
| Department of Consumer Affairs        | Issued on: 15.01.2023    | Prepared by :                    |
|                                       | Rev. Date:               | Reviewed by :                    |
|                                       | Rev. No.: 00             | Approved by :             Page-1 |
+---------------------------------------+--------------------------+----------------------------------+

1. Background
        To comply with the Legal Metrology Rules.

2. Purpose:
        The purpose is to lay down, the standard procedure, guidelines to be followed by
the industry and by the Director, Controller or any Legal Metrology Officer authorized by
or under the Act, to determine the net quantity of commodity (Edible Oils & Fats)
contained in any package.

3. Scope:
        This document & procedure is applicable whenever the Director, Controller or any
Legal Metrology Officer authorized by or under the Act, draws any sample from any
manufacturing plant /in transit/ CFA/market for determination of the net quantity of
packed Commodity (edible oils & fats) or to confirm any other requirement specified
under Legal Metrology Rules/guidelines /advisories.

PROCEDURE:

        Determination of the Net Quantity of Commodities in Packages
                            Part-I - Equipment
1. Equipments required- (1) The Director, Controller or any Legal Metrology Officer
authorized by or under the Act, to determine the net quantity; of commodity contained
in any package, shall be provided by the concerned Government with adequate
equipments and facilities to carry out his work to the required degree of accuracy in
an Expedient and efficient manner.
(2) The equipments, referred to in sub-paragraph (1), shall ordinarily consist of working
standard weights and balances, or any other weight or measure declared by the Director
to be suitable for determining the net quantity contained in the concerned package.
(3) The Director, Controller or any Legal Metrology Officer shall, before determining the
net quantity contained in any package, ensure that the working standards and other
equipments are functioning properly.

2. Working standards to be ordinarily used.-(1) Ordinarily, working standards and
other weights and measures, duly verified shall be used for determining the net
quantities contained in packages and the errors, if any, in relation to the net quantity
declared on the package.
Provided that, where it is necessary to use the weights and measures, owned or
controlled by the manufacturer, packer or wholesale dealer as the case may be such
weights and measures, shall be used unless the maximum permissible error of such
weights and measures is twenty per cent or less of the maximum error permitted in
relation to the net quantity of the commodity contained in the packages.
(2) It shall be the duty of every manufacturer, packer or wholesale dealer to render
such assistance the Director, Controller or any Legal Metrology Officer as the Director
or other authorized person may require in order carrying out his duties expeditiously and
efficiently.
(3) Where any weight, measure or other equipment, owned or controlled by the
manufacturer, packer or wholesale dealer is used by the Director, Controller or any
Legal Metrology Officer for the determination or the net quantity contained in any
package, such weight, measure or other equipment shall not be released by the Director
or other authorized person until his work is completed.

        Part-II - Instructions with regard to the determination of quantity and error
                        at manufacturer's or packer's premises.
3. (1) If empty tare packages are available, one such package shall be taken and tare
package weight determined. If the tare package weight is equal to or less than three-
tenths of the maximum permissible error for the concerned commodity of that quantity,
it shall be assumed that the tare package weight so determined is valid for all the
other packages in the sample, and the sample packages shall then be weighed for
gross weight, and thereafter the net weight of commodity contained in each package
shall be obtained by subtracting the tare package weight from the gross package
weight. If the tare package weight so determined is more than three tenths
of the maximum permissible error in relation to the commodity for that quantity, weight
of four emptier tare packages shall be determined. If the difference between the
maximum tare package weight and the minimum tare package weight of the five
samples is equal to or less than four tenths of the maximum permissible error allowed
for that commodity for that quantity, the tare weight of the package shall be taken as
equal to the average of the five tare package weights. The gross weight of the package
in the sample shall then be determined and the average tare package weight shall be
subtracted from the gross package weight to obtain the net contents of the commodity
in each package, in the sample. However, when the difference in the five individual tare
weights of the packages exceeds the limit of four tenths of the maximum permissible
error specified above, the procedure specified in sub-paragraph (4) or sub-paragraph
(5), as the case may be, shall be adopted for determining the net quantity of individual
packages.
(2) In the absence of empty tare packages, one package from the sample shall be
opened and the net weight of the commodity and the tare weight of the package
determined. If the tare weight of the package is equal to or less than three-tenth of the
maximum permissible error for that concerned commodity of that quantity, it shall be
assumed that the tare weight of one package so determined is valid for all the other
packages in the sample, and the remaining packages shall then be weighed for gross
weight, and thereafter the net weight of commodity contained in each package shall be
obtained by subtracting the tare weight from the gross weight.
(3) If the tare weight of the package specified in sub-paragraph (2) is more than
three-tenths of the maximum permissible error in relation to that commodity, four
more packages in the samples shall be opened and the net weight of the commodity
in each package as well as the tare weight of each package shall be determined. If the
difference between the maximum tare weight and the minimum tare weight of five
samples is equal to or less than four-tenths of the maximum permissible error for that
commodity of that quantity, the tare weight of the package shall be taken as equal to
the arithmetic mean of the five tare weights. The gross weight of the packages in the
sample shall then be determined to the requisite accuracy and the average tare weight
of the contained shall be subtracted from the gross weight to obtain the net contents of
the commodity in each package in the sample.
(4) Where it is found on examining the first package, as described in sub-paragraph (2)
that the tare weight of the container exceeds the limits specified in sub-paragraph (2)
and sub-paragraph (3) and the determination of net contents, cannot be carried out
without opening the remaining packages, or it is not, possible to use the procedure to
use the procedure described in sub-paragraph (5) the size of the sample to be
examined shall be restricted to that indicated in column (2) , in accordance with the
lot indicated in column (1) of Table of the Fifth Schedule and the determination of net
quantity shall be carried out by opening all the packages in the sample.
(5) Where it is likely that the process of determination of the actual net quantity may be
destructive and it is possible to feed empty package into the packing process without
affecting the results, the following procedure shall be used:
(a) the number of empty packages, depending upon the size of the lot, in accordance
with columns (1) and (2) of Table of the Fifth Schedule shall be selected;
(b) the empty package shall be marked suitably to identify them from the other
packages;
(c) the weight of each empty package, with its other parts which are to be fitted on it
after it is filled shall be determined and the weight suitably recorded on the empty
package and also on the Form specified in the Seventh Schedule;
(d) the empty package shall then be filled by introducing them in a random manner in
the packing process, and such introduction shall be adequately spread over the
duration in accordance with which ;the size of the lot is determined;
(e) the marked packages shall be taken out after completion of the filling and sealing
operations and each such filled package shall be weighed again to the requisite
accuracy;
(f) the net quantity shall be obtained by deducting the tare weight determined in
accordance with clause (c) from the gross weight.
(6) The Director, Controller or any Legal Metrology Officer shall enter results of this
examination of gross weight, net weight and tare weight in the Form specified in the
Seventh Schedule along with such other observation as he may wish to make on the
basis of his examination.

4. Determination of (Edible oils & Fats) Volumetric packed commodities Net
Contents by declared weight on Pack. -(1) In case of edible oils & fats being
packed in volumetric packs, the net quantity shall be estimated by their equivalent
weight only. The standard specific gravity conversion factors to be adopted for:
(a) soft oils shall be 910 g for 1Litre at 30 deg C,
(b) other than soft oils like palm-based oils & fats, vanaspati etc. shall be 893 g for 1 Litre at 50 deg C,
(c) ghee shall be 900g at 45 deg C.
The procedure adopted for measuring weight of edibles oils & fats shall be
applied here as well.

(2) The reading of the actual net declared weight the Edible oils & Fat's commodity in
every package shall be noted carefully and recorded in the Form specified in the
Seventh Schedule.

5. Declaration of unit sale price: The unit sale price required under rule 6 of the
Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 shall mandatorily be declared in
weight. However, it may be declared additionally in volume also.

                                THE SEVENTH SCHEDULE
                                        Form A
                            Weight Checking – Data Sheet

A. Particulars of Package:
   Name of Manufacturer/ Packer:
   Address:                Price:          Month:          Year:

B. Commodity Classification:
   Lot Size:                               Sample Size:
   Schedule First/ Second:
   Class A/ B:
   Maximum permissible error in percentage:
   *Lot size:              Sample size:            Maximum permissible error (g)

C. Weight Checking Data:
   Sample No. (1 to 200) | Gross Weight | Tare Weight | Net Weight/ error | Remarks

D. Results:
   Declared Wt.                            Avg. Wt.

E. GENERAL COMMENTS WITH REGARD TO THE COMPLIANCE WITH THE ACT AND THE RULES
   MADE THEREUNDER:

F. Signature and name of the authorized person:
   Designation / Name / Time:
   Signature and name of manufacturer/packer authorized by manufacturer or any competent witness:
   Place:

                                                                        (Ashutosh Agarwal)
                                                      Deputy Director & I/c Director (Legal Metrology)
                                              Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution
                                                          Department of Consumer Affairs
                                                    Govt. of India Krishi Bhawan, New Delhi-110001
                                                                        Date: 29/12/2023
```

# Complete Extraction: The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Amendment) Rules, 2011 & Statutory Advisory on Investigational Enforcement (G.S.R. 318(E) & F. No. WM-9(6)/2010-Pt.)

**Source Files:**
1. `dataset/8(i)_0_1732707804.pdf` — Gazette Notification G.S.R. 318(E)
2. `dataset/advisory_pcr(1)_0_1732707749.pdf` — Statutory Implementation Advisory dated 29.04.2011

**Document Classification:** Statutory Gazette Amendment Rules & Executive Implementation Guidelines  
**Gazette Registration No.:** REGD. NO. D. L.-33004/99 | No. 205, New Delhi, Wednesday, April 13, 2011 / Chaitra 23, 1933  
**Notification Number:** G.S.R. 318(E) / सा.का.नि. 318(अ)  
**Date of Gazette Publication:** 13th April, 2011  
**Statutory Authority:** Central Government, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Consumer Affairs)  
**Parent Enabling Act:** Section 52(1) read with Section 52(2)(j) of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 (1 of 2010)  
**Parent Rules:** The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 (published vide G.S.R. 202(E) dated 7th March, 2011, effective 1st April, 2011)  
**Signatory (Gazette):** Rakesh Kacker, Special Secretary [F. No. WM-9(6)/2010-Pt.] / राकेश कक्कड़, विशेष सचिव [फा. सं. डब्ल्यू एम-9(6)/2010-पार्ट]  
**Advisory File Reference:** F. No. WM-9(6)/2010-Pt., dated 29.04.2011, Krishi Bhawan, New Delhi  
**Signatory (Advisory):** B. N. Dixit, Director (Legal Metrology), approved by Secretary, Consumer Affairs  
**Status in Repository:** 100% Comprehensive Verbatim Extraction (Bilingual English & Hindi) with In-Depth Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations

---

## 1. Executive Summary & Statutory Impact

### Historical Context & Immediate Legislative Need:
When the **Legal Metrology Act, 2009 (1 of 2010)** was brought into total force on **1st April, 2011** (vide S.O. 210(E)), replacing the erstwhile Standards of Weights and Measures Act, 1976 and the Standards of Weights and Measures (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 1977, brand owners, packagers, and manufacturing federations faced severe logistical disruption. Huge volumes of pre-printed packaging materials, cartons, wrappers, and containers procured prior to 31st March, 2011 under the 1977 regulatory regime were lying unexhausted in industrial inventories across India.

Under strict interpretation of the newly enacted **Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011**, utilizing packaging that lacked newly phrased statutory declarations or contact particulars would expose businesses to immediate prosecution, seizure of commodities under Section 15, and compounding/criminal penalties under Section 36.

### Two-Pronged Government Intervention:
To avert widespread destruction of viable packaging materials and industrial paralysis while ensuring consumers received accurate information, the Central Government executed two coordinated statutory instruments:

1. **Promulgation of G.S.R. 318(E) (The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Amendment) Rules, 2011):**
   - Published in the Gazette of India on **13th April, 2011**.
   - Substantively amended **Rule 6** by inserting a new sub-rule **(6)**.
   - Authorized manufacturers and packers to exhaust existing packaging inventory up to **30th September, 2011** (a 6-month transition period) or until stocks were exhausted, whichever occurred earlier.
   - Mandated that necessary statutory corrections must be made on the wrappers/packages by way of **rubber stamping**, **affixing stickers**, or **online printing**.

2. **Issuance of Executive Implementation Guidelines (Advisory WM-9(6)/2010-Pt. dated 29.04.2011):**
   - Addressed to Controllers of Legal Metrology across all States and Union Territories.
   - Directed State enforcement machinery to restrict early compliance checks to **"investigational surveys"** only.
   - Instructed officers to notify businesses of deficiencies rather than instituting penal proceedings or seizures.
   - Formally established a **statutory moratorium on prosecutions** during the transition window up to 30th September, 2011.

---

## 2. Complete Verbatim Statutory Text of G.S.R. 318(E)

### English Gazette Version:

```text
THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY
PART II—Section 3—Sub-section (i)
PUBLISHED BY AUTHORITY

No. 205]    NEW DELHI, WEDNESDAY, APRIL 13, 2011/CHAITRA 23, 1933

MINISTRY OF CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION
(Department of Consumer Affairs)
NOTIFICATION
New Delhi, the 13th April, 2011

G.S.R. 318(E).—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) read with clause (j) of sub-section (2) of Section 52 of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009, (1 of 2010) the Central Government hereby makes the following rules to amend the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011, namely :—

1. (1) These rules may be called the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Amendment) Rules, 2011.
   (2) They shall come into force on the date of their publication in the Official Gazette.

2. In the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 in rule 6, after sub-rule (5), the following shall be inserted, namely :—

   "(6) Any packaging material or wrapper which could not be exhausted by the manufacturer or packer may be used for packing of the material up to 30th September, 2011 or till such date the packaging material or wrapper is exhausted, whichever is earlier, after making the corrections required under these rules by way of stamping or putting sticker or online printing, as the case may be."

[F. No. WM-9(6)/2010-Pt.]
RAKESH KACKER, Spl. Secy.

Note : The Principal rules were published in Gazette of India on 9th March, 2011, vide G.S.R. 202 (E), dated 7th March, 2011.
```

```text
सा.का.नि. 318(अ).—केन्द्रीय सरकार, विधिक माप विज्ञान अधिनियम, 2009 (2010 का 1) की धारा 52 की उप-धारा (2) के खण्ड (च) के साथ पठित उप-धारा (1) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) नियम, 2011 का संशोधन करने के लिए निम्नलिखित नियम बनाती है, अर्थात्:—

1. (1) इन नियमों का संक्षिप्त नाम विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) (संशोधन) नियम, 2011 है।
   (2) ये राजपत्र में प्रकाशन की तारीख को प्रवृत्त होंगे।

2. विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) नियम, 2011 के नियम 6 के उप-नियम (5) के पश्चात् निम्नलिखित रखा जाएगा, अर्थात्:—

   "(6) कोई भी पैकिंग सामग्री या रैपर जिसको विनिर्माता या पैकर द्वारा समाप्त नहीं किया जा सका हो, यथास्थिति स्टैम्प लगाकर या स्टीकर लगाकर या ऑनलाइन मुद्रण द्वारा, इन नियमों के अधीन अपेक्षित सुधार करने के पश्चात् 30 सितम्बर, 2011 तक या पैकिंग सामग्री या रैपर के समाप्त होने की तारीख तक, इसमें से जो पूर्वतर हो, प्रयोग किया जा सकेगा।"

[फा. सं. डब्ल्यू एम-9(6)/2010-पार्ट]
```

## 3. Verbatim Extraction of Implementation Guidelines / Advisory (WM-9(6)/2010-Pt. dated 29.04.2011)

```text
WM- 9(6)/2010-Pt.
Government of India
Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food & Public Distribution
Department of Consumer Affairs

Krishi Bhawan, New Delhi
Dated: 29.4.2011

To
The Controller of Legal Metrology,
All States/UTs

Subject: Implementation of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 and the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011– Guidelines for implementation thereof.

Sir/ Madam,

    As you are aware that the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 have been implemented along with the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 w.e.f. 1-4-2011.

2.  On the request of the Industries Associations, that industries are not able to utilize their existing old packaging material upto 31-3-2011 and is lying with them, the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 was amended vide GSR No.318(E) dated 13th April, 2011 allowing them to utilize their packaging material or wrapper which could not be exhausted by the manufacturer or packer may be used for packing of the material upto 30th September, 2011 or till such date the packaging material of wrapper is exhausted, whichever is earlier, after making the corrections required under these rules by way of stamping or putting sticker or online printing, as the case may be.

3.  Since the industries are representing that they need a lead time to change from old rules to new rules to update the labeling requirements, it is requested that

    (i) Efforts may be made to give vide publicity to the changes made in the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 so as to spread awareness amongst manufacturers/packers etc.

    (ii) The initial enforcement steps may only be in the nature of investigational surveys. Any deficiency noticed should be brought in the notice of the concerned. This is being suggested to provide an opportunity to manufacturers/packers to update their label declarations. This approach may be continued upto 30th September, 2011 and it is expected that in this initial period there should be no prosecution.

    (iii) The Enforcement Authorities of your State may accordingly be instructed for due compliance of the above guidelines.

    This issues with the approval of Secretary, Consumer Affairs.

                                                            Yours faithfully,
                                                            (B N Dixit)
                                                            Director (Legal Metrology)
                                                            Ph:011-23389489/ 23386194,
                                                            Fax-011-23385322

Copy to: All Major Industry Associations
```

## 4. Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations & Doctrinal Architecture

### A. Statutory Interlock with Parent Act:
- **Section 52(1) & 52(2)(j) of Legal Metrology Act, 2009:** Confers plenary regulation-making authority upon the Central Government to prescribe rules regulating pre-packaged commodities, including labeling, declarations, packaging forms, inspections, and tolerances.
- **Rule 6 of PCR 2011:** Establishes the foundational statutory charter of mandatory declarations that must appear on every pre-packaged commodity (manufacturer name & address, net quantity, month/year of manufacture/import, consumer care details, retail sale price/MRP).
- **Rule 6(6) Insertion:** Directly addresses transitional friction. In administrative law, retroactive or immediate statutory changes creating economic waste without transition windows violate principles of proportionality and legitimate expectation. Rule 6(6) created a lawful bridge.

### B. The Three Authorized Rectification Modes:
Rule 6(6) strictly circumscribed how unexhausted packaging materials could be legally salvaged:
1. **Rubber Stamping:** Manual stamping with indelible ink of updated details (e.g., consumer care telephone/email, updated MRP, or amended legal declarations).
2. **Affixing Stickers:** Overlay adhesive labels containing the mandatory declarations required under PCR 2011 that were absent on the old wrapper.
3. **Online Printing:** Utilizing continuous inkjet (CIJ) or thermal transfer overprinting (TTO) directly on the packing line to print mandatory fields directly onto the wrapper during packaging.

### C. The Doctrine of "Investigational Surveys" vs. "Prosecution":
The advisory dated 29.04.2011 from Director B. N. Dixit introduces a landmark doctrine in Indian metrological enforcement:
- **Investigational Surveys:** Field inspections where Legal Metrology Inspectors inspect retail premises and depots not to register seizure memos or initiate criminal complaints under Section 36, but to audit labels, issue advisory notices, and document non-compliances.
- **Moratorium on Prosecution:** Explicit instruction that *“there should be no prosecution”* until 30th September, 2011. This protected industry from arbitrary harassment and rent-seeking by local inspectors while maintaining federal supervisory discipline.

### D. Downstream Extension History:
Although G.S.R. 318(E) fixed the initial cutoff date as **30th September, 2011**, industry subsequently demonstrated that global supply chain lead times and procurement volumes prevented complete exhaustion within 6 months. Consequently:
- **G.S.R. 734(E) dated 30th September, 2011 (Document 66):** Substituted *"upto 30th September, 2011"* with *"upto 31st March, 2012"*, granting an additional 6 months of transitional protection.

---

## 5. Comprehensive Q&A Reference for SIH Problem Statement 26035

### Q1: What was the primary purpose of G.S.R. 318(E) dated 13th April, 2011?
**Answer:** To amend Rule 6 of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 by inserting sub-rule (6), which permitted manufacturers and packers to utilize unexhausted packaging material and wrappers procured prior to the 1st April 2011 enforcement date up to 30th September, 2011, provided necessary statutory corrections were made via stamping, stickers, or online printing.

### Q2: What three methods were legally permitted under Rule 6(6) to correct pre-printed packaging inventory?
**Answer:** 
1. Stamping (stamping required corrections with indelible ink);
2. Putting stickers (affixing adhesive correction labels);
3. Online printing (direct packaging line printing/CIJ/laser marking).

### Q3: What policy instruction did the Central Government issue to State Controllers regarding enforcement during the transition window?
**Answer:** Via Statutory Advisory WM-9(6)/2010-Pt. dated 29.04.2011, the Central Government instructed all State Controllers that initial enforcement actions must be limited to *investigational surveys* to create awareness and point out deficiencies to manufacturers/packers, with an explicit direction that *there should be no prosecution* during the transition period ending 30th September, 2011.

### Q4: Who signed G.S.R. 318(E) and the accompanying advisory?
**Answer:** 
- G.S.R. 318(E) was signed by **Rakesh Kacker**, Special Secretary to the Government of India.
- The implementation advisory was signed by **B. N. Dixit**, Director (Legal Metrology), with the approval of the Secretary, Consumer Affairs.

---

**Notification Number:** G.S.R. 734(E) / सा.का.नि. 734(अ)  
**Date of Gazette Publication:** 30th September, 2011  
**Statutory Authority:** Central Government, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Consumer Affairs)  
**Parent Enabling Act:** Section 52(1) read with Section 52(2)(j) of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 (1 of 2010)  
**Parent Rules:** The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 (published vide G.S.R. 202(E) dated 7th March, 2011, and amended vide G.S.R. 318(E) dated 13th April, 2011)  
**Signatory:** Pankaj Agarwala, Additional Secretary [F. No. WM-9(6)/2010-Pt.] / पंकज अग्रवाल, अपर सचिव [फा. सं. डब्ल्यू एम-9(6)/2010-पार्ट]  
**Status in Repository:** 100% Comprehensive Verbatim Extraction (Bilingual English & Hindi) with In-Depth Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations

---

## 1. Executive Summary & Statutory Impact

### Immediate Regulatory Context:
On **13th April, 2011**, the Central Government promulgated **G.S.R. 318(E)** (The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Amendment) Rules, 2011), inserting **Rule 6(6)** into the newly enacted PCR 2011. This gave manufacturers and packers permission to utilize unexhausted packaging materials and wrappers procured under the repealed 1977 regulatory regime up to **30th September, 2011**, subject to mandatory corrections made by stamping, stickers, or online printing.

As the 30th September, 2011 deadline arrived, major industrial bodies and FMCG manufacturers submitted formal representations to the Department of Consumer Affairs establishing that:
1. Significant quantities of pre-printed wrappers, metal cans, cartons, and corrugated boxes were still in inventory due to long procurement lead times, minimum order quantities, and seasonality in production cycles.
2. Immediate obsolescence would cause devastating financial losses running into hundreds of crores and result in the destruction of usable packaging materials.
3. Consumers were already protected because manufacturers were actively applying corrective stickers and stamps reflecting the newly required PCR 2011 declarations.

### Statutory Remedy Executed by G.S.R. 734(E):
On the exact expiry date of the initial window (**30th September, 2011**), the Central Government issued **G.S.R. 734(E)**:
- Substituted the cutoff date in **Rule 6(6)** from *"upto 30th September, 2011"* to **"upto 31st March, 2012"**.
- Granted a vital **6-month additional extension** to industry to exhaust pre-existing packaging inventories.
- Kept all other statutory conditions intact: rectifications by rubber stamp, sticker, or online printing remained mandatory.
- Reaffirmed the legal continuity established under Section 52(2)(j) of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009.

## 3. Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations & Legislative Architecture

### A. Statutory Mechanics of the Amendment:
- **Amended Provision:** Rule 6, sub-rule (6) of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011.
- **Pre-Amendment Text of Rule 6(6):**
  > *"Any packaging material or wrapper which could not be exhausted by the manufacturer or packer may be used for packing of the material up to 30th September, 2011 or till such date the packaging material or wrapper is exhausted, whichever is earlier, after making the corrections required under these rules by way of stamping or putting sticker or online printing, as the case may be."*
- **Post-Amendment Text of Rule 6(6):**
  > *"Any packaging material or wrapper which could not be exhausted by the manufacturer or packer may be used for packing of the material up to 31st March, 2012 or till such date the packaging material or wrapper is exhausted, whichever is earlier, after making the corrections required under these rules by way of stamping or putting sticker or online printing, as the case may be."*

### B. Doctrinal and Operational Significance:
1. **Prevention of Waste vs. Consumer Information Symmetry:**
   The Central Government balanced the economic burden on manufacturers with the consumer's right to accurate labeling. Rather than forcing the destruction of packaging, the law permitted transitional use provided that mandatory details under PCR 2011 (such as consumer care address/telephone/email, retail price, and net weight declarations) were physically added via stickers, rubber stamping, or online printing.
2. **Harmonization with Enforcement Policy:**
   This statutory amendment formed the legal backbone enabling State Controllers to continue the policy of investigational surveys and non-prosecution for old packaging materials as originally formulated under Advisory WM-9(6)/2010-Pt.
3. **Signatory Hierarchy:**
   Signed by **Pankaj Agarwala**, Additional Secretary to the Government of India, reflecting the high-level policy review conducted within the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food & Public Distribution.

---

## 4. Comprehensive Q&A Reference for SIH Problem Statement 26035

### Q1: What specific change was enacted by G.S.R. 734(E) dated 30th September, 2011?
**Answer:** G.S.R. 734(E) amended sub-rule (6) of Rule 6 of the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 by extending the deadline for utilizing unexhausted packaging materials and wrappers from **30th September, 2011** to **31st March, 2012**.

### Q2: What was the formal title of G.S.R. 734(E)?
**Answer:** *The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Second Amendment Rules, 2011* (विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) द्वितीय संशोधन नियम, 2011).

### Q3: What mandatory conditions had to be fulfilled by a manufacturer or packer to utilize unexhausted packaging material up to 31st March, 2012?
**Answer:** The manufacturer or packer was legally required to make all corrections mandated under the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 onto the packaging material or wrapper by means of:
1. Stamping with indelible ink;
2. Putting stickers (adhesive overlay labels); or
3. Online printing (continuous inkjet / thermal transfer printing during production).

### Q4: Who was the signatory to G.S.R. 734(E) and under what file reference was it issued?
**Answer:** G.S.R. 734(E) was signed by **Pankaj Agarwala**, Additional Secretary to the Government of India, under file reference **[F. No. WM-9(6)/2010-Pt.]**.

# Complete Extraction: The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Third Amendment Rules, 2011 (G.S.R. 784(E))

**Source File:** `dataset/8(iii)_0_1732707869.pdf`

**Gazette Registration No.:** REGD. NO. D. L.-33004/99 | No. 591, New Delhi, Tuesday, October 25, 2011 / Kartika 3, 1933  
**Notification Number:** G.S.R. 784(E) / सा.का.नि. 784(अ)  
**Date of Notification:** 24th October, 2011 (Published in Gazette: 25th October, 2011)  
**Enforcement Date:** 1st July, 2012  
**Statutory Authority:** Central Government, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Consumer Affairs)  
**Parent Enabling Act:** Section 52(1) read with Section 52(2)(j) of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 (1 of 2010)  
**Parent Rules:** The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 (published vide G.S.R. 202(E) dated 7th March, 2011, and last amended vide G.S.R. 734(E) dated 30th September, 2011)  
**Signatory:** Manoj Kumar Parida, Joint Secretary [F. No. WM-11(13)/2010] / मनोज कुमार परिड़ा, संयुक्त सचिव [फा. सं. डब्ल्यू एम-11(13)/2010]  
**Related Corrigendum:** G.S.R. 858(E) dated 29th November, 2011 (Document 12)  
**Status in Repository:** 100% Comprehensive Verbatim Extraction (Bilingual English & Hindi) with In-Depth Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations

---

## 1. Executive Summary & Statutory Impact

### Foundational Nature of G.S.R. 784(E):
Promulgated on **24th October, 2011** and published on **25th October, 2011**, **G.S.R. 784(E)** introduced six fundamental substantive and procedural reforms to the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011. While earlier amendments (G.S.R. 318(E) and G.S.R. 734(E)) dealt with transitional runway extensions for unexhausted packaging stocks, G.S.R. 784(E) restructured core operational rules governing retail declarations, enforcement seizures, and scheduled commodities.

To allow industries ample time to re-engineer labels, packaging lines, and quality assurance workflows, the Central Government deliberately established an **eight-month advance implementation date of 1st July, 2012**.

### Key Statutory Pillars of G.S.R. 784(E):

1. **Absolute Prohibition of Misleading / Exaggerated Net Quantity Declarations (Rule 12(6)):**
   Replaced Rule 12(6) with an ironclad prohibition: No package may bear any word, expression, prefix, or suffix of any sort whatsoever that tends to create or is likely to create an exaggerated, misleading, or inadequate impression regarding the quantity contained (e.g., prohibiting misleading qualifiers like "Jumbo Litre", "Full Kilo", or deceptively oversized package fonts).

2. **Establishment of Factory / Depot Level Primary Enforcement Point (Rule 19(7)):**
   Substituted Rule 19(7) to prescribe that enforcement compliance for mandatory declarations must be verified and ensured primarily at the point of origin—namely, **either at the factory level or at the depot of the factory**. This protected intermediate distributors, wholesalers, and small retail shopkeepers from harassment for pre-printed factory packaging omissions.

3. **Statutory Cap on Evidence Seizures — Five Representative Samples Rule (Rule 19(8)):**
   Revolutionized search and seizure under Legal Metrology enforcement:
   - For non-compliance with declaration rules, inspecting officers may seize **only five representative samples** of the packages as evidence for legal proceedings.
   - The remaining bulk stock of packages cannot be indefinitely locked up or destroyed; instead, they **must be released** once compliance (e.g., corrective relabeling or stamping) is completed by the manufacturer or packer.

4. **Streamlining Exemptions and Provisos:**
   - Omitted the proviso to **Rule 5** (regulation of pack sizes).
   - Omitted the third proviso to **Rule 6(1)(d)**.
   - Omitted the proviso to **Rule 26(a)** (small package exemptions).

5. **Fourth Schedule Unit Correction for Item 15:**
   In the Fourth Schedule, against serial number 15 (governing specific commodities), corrected the prescribed unit of net quantity in column 3 from *"volume"* to **"weight"**.

```text
THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : EXTRAORDINARY
PART II—Section 3—Sub-section (i)
PUBLISHED BY AUTHORITY

No. 591]    NEW DELHI, TUESDAY, OCTOBER 25, 2011/KARTIKA 3, 1933

(Department of Consumer Affairs)

NOTIFICATION
New Delhi, the 24th October, 2011

G.S.R. 784(E).—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) read with clause (j) of sub-section (2) of Section 52 of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 (1 of 2010), the Central Government hereby makes the following rules further to amend the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011, namely :—

1. (1) These rules may be called as the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Third Amendment Rules, 2011.
   (2) They shall come into force with effect from 1st July, 2012.

2. In the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011 (hereinafter referred to as the principal rules),—

   (a) the proviso to the Rule 5 shall be omitted.

   (b) in clause (d), sub-rule (1) third proviso shall be omitted.
       [Note: Clarified by G.S.R. 858(E) as "in clause (d) of sub-rule (1) of rule 6, the third proviso shall be omitted."]

   (c) in rule 12, for sub-rule (6), the following sub-rule shall be substituted, namely :—
       "(6) the declaration of the quantity under these rules shall not contain any word or expression, of any sort whatsoever, which tends to create or is likely to create an exaggerated, misleading or inadequate expression as to the quantity of the commodity contained in the package.",

   (d) in rule 19, for sub-rule (7), the following sub-rule shall be substituted, namely :—
       "(7) the requirement of mandatory declarations on packages shall be ensured either at the factory level and at the depot of the factory.",
       [Note: "and at" corrected to "or at" vide Corrigendum G.S.R. 858(E)]

   (e) in rule 19, for sub-rule (8), the following sub-rule shall be substituted, namely :—
       "(8) For non-compliance of the provisions of this rule, action may be taken after seizing five representative samples of the packages as evidence and the rest of the packages may be released only after compliance is completed by the manufacturer or the packer, as the case may be.",

   (f) in rule 26, in clause (a), the proviso shall be omitted.

   (g) in the Forth Schedule, for the entry "volume" in column 3 against serial number 15, the following entry shall be substituted, namely :—
       "weight".

[F. No. WM-11(13)/2010]
MANOJ KUMAR PARIDA, Jt. Secy.

Note : The principal rules were published in the Gazette of India, vide notification number G.S.R. 202(E), dated 7th March, 2011 and last amended, vide, notification number G.S.R. 734(E), dated 30th September, 2011.
```

```text
   (ख) खंड (घ) में, उप-नियम (1) के तृतीय परन्तुक का लोप किया जाएगा।
       [टिप्पण: शुद्धिपत्र सा.का.नि. 858(अ) द्वारा "नियम 6 के उप-नियम (1) के खंड (घ) में, तृतीय परन्तुक का लोप किया जाएगा" स्पष्टीकृत]

   (ग) नियम 12 में, उप-नियम (6) के स्थान पर, निम्नलिखित उप-नियम रखा जाएगा, अर्थात् :—
       "(6) इन नियमों के अधीन मात्रा की घोषणा में किसी भी प्रकार का ऐसा कोई शब्द अथवा पद अंतर्विष्ट नहीं होगा जिससे पैकेज में अंतर्विष्ट वस्तु की मात्रा के बारे में बढ़ा-चढ़ा कर, भ्रामक या अपर्याप्त प्रभाव पड़े या पड़ने की सम्भावना हो।",

   (घ) नियम 19 में, उप-नियम (7) के स्थान पर निम्नलिखित उप-नियम रखा जाएगा, अर्थात् :—
       "(7) पैकेजों पर अनिवार्य घोषणाओं की अपेक्षा फैक्ट्री स्तर पर और फैक्ट्री के डिपो के स्तर पर सुनिश्चित की जाएगी।",
       [टिप्पण: शुद्धिपत्र द्वारा "फैक्ट्री स्तर पर अथवा फैक्ट्री के डिपो के स्तर पर" संशोधित]

   (ङ) नियम 19 में, उप-नियम (8) के स्थान पर निम्नलिखित उप-नियम रखा जाएगा, अर्थात् :—
       "(8) इस नियम के उपबंधों का अनुपालन न करने पर, इन पैकेजों के पाँच प्रतिनिधि नमूनों को अभिग्रहण कर साक्ष्य के रूप में कार्रवाई की जा सकेगी और शेष पैकेजों को, यथास्थिति, निर्माता अथवा पैकर द्वारा अनुपालन पूरा करने के पश्चात् ही छोड़ा जाएगा।",

   (च) नियम 26 में खंड (क) के परन्तुक का लोप किया जाएगा।

   (छ) चतुर्थ अनुसूची में स्तंभ 3 के क्रम संख्यांक 15 के सामने की प्रविष्टि "आयतन" के स्थान पर निम्नलिखित प्रविष्टि रखी जाएगी, अर्थात् :—
       "वजन"।

[फा. सं. डब्ल्यू एम-11(13)/2010]

```

---

## 3. Deep-Dive Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations

### A. The "Five Representative Samples" Doctrine (Rule 19(8)):
Prior to this amendment, inspecting Legal Metrology Officers often seized entire consignments, truckloads, or warehouse inventories of pre-packaged goods when labeling non-compliances (such as missing consumer care emails or incorrect font heights) were discovered. This caused enormous commercial hardship, inventory degradation, and extortion risks.

Rule 19(8) instituted a statutory balancing principle:
1. **Evidentiary Seizure Cap:** Officers are legally restricted to seizing **five representative sample packages** to retain as physical evidence in court or compounding proceedings under Section 36 of the Act.
2. **Mandatory Release Mechanism:** The remaining stock of packages cannot be confiscated or destroyed for technical labeling faults; the enforcement authority is statutorily bound to **release the remaining packages** once the manufacturer or packer completes rectifications (e.g., through relabeling or applying authorized correction stickers).

### B. Factory / Depot Level Verification (Rule 19(7)):
Sub-rule (7) establishes that the primary duty of ensuring mandatory declarations lies at the source of packaging—at the **factory level or at the factory's authorized depot**. This policy directive shields downstream bona fide retailers who sell sealed, intact factory packages from unfair prosecutions for factory printing defects, aligning with the principle of *mens rea* and supply chain attribution.

### C. Prevention of Misleading Net Quantity Descriptors (Rule 12(6)):
Rule 12(6) directly protects consumer cognitive fairness. Packagers frequently attempted to add promotional hyperbole to quantity statements, such as:
- *"Mega 1 Litre"* or *"Super 500g"*
- *"Gross weight 1050g including luxury packaging"*
- Statements confusing net drained weight with packaging weight.
Under Rule 12(6), the quantity declaration must be pure, clean, and numerical with the standard unit of measurement, completely devoid of puffery, exaggerations, or deceptive qualifiers.

### D. Interlock with Document 12 (Corrigendum G.S.R. 858(E)):
G.S.R. 784(E) contained minor clerical printing errors when published in October 2011:
1. Rule 2(b) referred to "in clause (d), sub-rule (1) third proviso" without explicitly naming Rule 6.
2. Rule 19(7) English text used "either at the factory level and at the depot" instead of "either at the factory level or at the depot".
On **29th November, 2011**, the Ministry issued **G.S.R. 858(E)** (Document 12 in this repository) specifically curing these typographical discrepancies.

---

## 4. Comprehensive Q&A Reference for SIH Problem Statement 26035

### Q1: What is the significance of Rule 19(8) as substituted by G.S.R. 784(E)?
**Answer:** Rule 19(8) legally restricts enforcement officers investigating labeling non-compliances to seizing **only five representative samples** of packages as evidence for prosecution, while mandating that the remainder of the seized consignment must be released to the manufacturer or packer upon completion of corrective compliance.

### Q2: How does Rule 12(6) regulate the net quantity declaration on packaged goods?
**Answer:** Rule 12(6) strictly prohibits any word or expression of any sort whatsoever that tends to create or is likely to create an exaggerated, misleading, or inadequate expression regarding the quantity of commodity contained in the package.

### Q3: When did the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Third Amendment Rules, 2011 come into force?
**Answer:** They came into force on **1st July, 2012**, providing industry an 8-month lead time from publication in October 2011.

### Q4: What change was made in the Fourth Schedule against serial number 15 by G.S.R. 784(E)?
**Answer:** The unit of measurement specified in column 3 was amended from **"volume"** to **"weight"**.

### Q5: Who signed G.S.R. 784(E) on behalf of the Central Government?
**Answer:** **Manoj Kumar Parida**, Joint Secretary to the Government of India, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution [F. No. WM-11(13)/2010].

## 3. Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations & Legislative Architecture

### A. Context and Genesis of G.S.R. 426(E)
1. **Background of G.S.R. 784(E) (24th October, 2011):**
   - In October 2011, the Central Government notified comprehensive amendments to the *Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011* under G.S.R. 784(E).
   - Under G.S.R. 784(E), substantial reforms were enacted, including:
     - Omission of the proviso to Rule 5 (which had previously permitted manufacturing and packaging of non-standard quantity sizes under certain conditions).
     - Introduction of Rule 19(8) (the five-sample investigation limit).
     - Overhaul of the Second Schedule (standardized packaging sizes).
   - The entire set of amendments in G.S.R. 784(E) was initially slated to come into force on **1st July, 2012**.

2. **Industry Representations & The Packaging Standard Transition Dilemma:**
   - As the 1st July, 2012 deadline approached, FMCG manufacturers, packaging machinery suppliers, and industry associations submitted representations to the Department of Consumer Affairs highlighting grave logistical bottlenecks.
   - The immediate omission of the proviso to Rule 5 meant that every packaged commodity listed in the Second Schedule would strictly have to be packed only in prescribed standard quantities (e.g., 50g, 100g, 200g, 500g, 1kg), with zero tolerance for intermediate or promotional sizes.
   - Re-tooling high-speed automated packaging machines, re-calibrating volumetric fillers, procuring newly sized cartons/foils, and clearing existing market inventory required additional transition time.

3. **Statutory Surgical Solution Enacted by G.S.R. 426(E):**
   - Issued on **5th June, 2012** (less than a month prior to the general enforcement date), G.S.R. 426(E) made a crucial bifurcated statutory adjustment:
     - **General Provisions:** Re-affirmed that the rest of G.S.R. 784(E) (including labelling requirements, Rule 19(8) inspection limits, Rule 12(6) prohibitions on confusing descriptors) would strictly take effect as scheduled on **1st July, 2012**.
     - **Rule 5 Proviso Omission Extension:** Granted an exclusive 4-month extension specifically for the omission of the proviso to Rule 5, shifting the omission date from **1st July, 2012 to 1st November, 2012**.

---

### B. Doctrinal Mechanics: Rule 5 Proviso Omission & Standard Packaging Sizes

| Statutory Milestone | Governing Notification | Statutory Effect on Rule 5 Proviso & Packaging Sizes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **7th March, 2011** | G.S.R. 202(E) | Principal Packaged Commodities Rules 2011 notified. Rule 5 mandated standard packaging sizes as specified in the Second Schedule, with a saving proviso allowing manufacturers to pack non-standard sizes for existing arrangements. |
| **24th October, 2011** | G.S.R. 784(E) | Rule 2(a) directed that the proviso to Rule 5 be omitted with effect from 1st July, 2012, thereby establishing mandatory standardization with zero exceptions. |
| **5th June, 2012** | **G.S.R. 426(E)** | Substituted Rule 2(a) of G.S.R. 784(E): **"The proviso to rule 5 shall be omitted from 1st November, 2012."** Provided an indispensable 4-month transition window for industry compliance. |
| **1st July, 2012** | G.S.R. 784(E) cum G.S.R. 426(E) | Core provisions of G.S.R. 784(E) became fully enforceable (Rule 19(8) 5-sample limit, Rule 12(6) descriptors, etc.). |
| **1st November, 2012** | G.S.R. 426(E) | Proviso to Rule 5 officially omitted. Full mandatory standard packaging under Second Schedule became absolute across India. |

---

## 4. Comprehensive Q&A Reference for SIH Problem Statement 26035

### Q1: What is the formal title and notification number of Document 68?
**Answer:** The formal title is **The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Second Amendment) Amendment Rules, 2012**, notified vide **G.S.R. 426(E)** [सा.का.नि. 426(अ)] dated **5th June, 2012**, published in Gazette of India Extraordinary No. 258.

### Q2: Which earlier notification was directly amended by G.S.R. 426(E)?
**Answer:** G.S.R. 426(E) specifically amended the **Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Third Amendment) Rules, 2011**, which had been notified vide **G.S.R. 784(E)** on 24th October, 2011.

### Q3: What specific bifurcated enforcement schedule was established by G.S.R. 426(E)?
**Answer:** G.S.R. 426(E) established that:
1. **General Rule:** Save as otherwise provided, the amendments introduced by G.S.R. 784(E) would come into force on **1st July, 2012**.
2. **Specific Exception:** The omission of the proviso to Rule 5 was deferred and would take effect from **1st November, 2012**.

### Q4: Why was the omission of the proviso to Rule 5 deferred from 1st July, 2012 to 1st November, 2012?
**Answer:** The proviso to Rule 5 had permitted manufacturers to package commodities in sizes other than the standard sizes prescribed in the Second Schedule. Deferring its omission until 1st November, 2012 gave industries an essential 4-month grace period to modify packaging machinery, adjust stock-keeping units (SKUs), and exhaust packaging materials conforming to non-standard quantities without facing prosecution under Section 36 of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009.

### Q5: Who was the signatory to G.S.R. 426(E), and under which official file was it processed?
**Answer:** G.S.R. 426(E) was signed by **Rajiv Agarwal**, Secretary to the Government of India, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution, and issued under file reference **`F. No. WM-11(13)/2010`**.

```text
New Delhi, the 6th June, 2013

G.S.R. 359(E).—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) read with clause (j) of sub-section (2) of Section 52 of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 (1 of 2010), the Central Government hereby makes the following rules further to amend the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011, namely :—

1. (1) These rules may be called as the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Amendment Rules, 2013.

2. In the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules, 2011—
   (a) in rule 2,—
       (i) after clause (b), the following clauses shall be inserted, namely :—
           "(bb) 'Industrial consumer' means the consumer who buys packaged commodities directly from the manufacturer for use by that industry;
            (bc) 'Institutional consumer' means any institution which hires or avails of the facilities or service in connection with transport, hotels, hospitals or such other service institutions which buy packaged commodities directly from the manufacturer for use by that institution;"
       (ii) in clause (k), the proviso shall be omitted;

   (b) in rule 3, the Explanation shall be omitted;

       "(8) (1) All the marketing companies, manufacturers, packers, importers or distributors of Liquefied Petroleum Gas cylinder shall maintain a check weigher or non-automatic weighing instrument, digital or analogue, of Accuracy class-III (Max. 50 kg, e=10g) to check the weight of the Liquefied Petroleum Gas cylinder.

         than the maximum retail price
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

       (a) against serial number 2, after the figures, letters and word, "5 kg and 10 kg", the figures and letters "75g, 125g, 150g, 250g," shall be inserted;
       (b) against serial number 3, after the figures and letter, "300g", the figures and letters "350g, 400g" shall be inserted;
       (c) against serial number 7, after the figures and letter "500g", the figures and letter "750g" shall be inserted;
       (d) against serial number 8, for the entry, the following entry shall be substituted, namely :—
           "Below 25g no restriction, 25g, 50g, 75g, 100g, 125g, 150g, 200g, 250g, 500g, 750g, 1kg, 1.5kg, 2kg and thereafter in the multiple of 1kg";
       (e) against serial number 9, after the figures and letter "100g", the figures and letter "125g" shall be inserted;
       (f) against serial number 10,—
           (i) after the word, figure and letters "multiple of 5kg" the words, figures and letters "below 50g no restriction, 175g, 300g, 750g" shall be inserted;
           (ii) after the word "numerals", the words figures and letters, "Below 50 ml no restriction, 175 ml, 300 ml, 750 ml" shall be inserted;
       (g) against serial number 12, after the figure and letters "2kg" the figures and letters "2.5kg, 3kg" shall be inserted;
       (h) against serial number 16, the brackets and words "(in can only)" shall be omitted.

[F. No. WM-10(6)/2013]
MANOJ KUMAR PARIDA, Jt. Secy.

Note:—The principal rules were published in the Gazette of India, Extraordinary, Part II, Section 3, Sub-section (i), vide number G.S.R. 202(E), dated the 7th March, 2011 and last amended, vide notification number G.S.R. 426(E), dated the 5th June, 2012.
```

## 3. Legal Metrology Analytical Annotations & Doctrinal Architecture

### A. The Codification of "Industrial Consumer" and "Institutional Consumer" (Rule 2(bb) & 2(bc))
1. **The Definitional Problem:**
   - Under Rule 3 of the original 2011 PCR, Chapter II declarations were made applicable to all packages intended for "retail sale".
   - Commercial enterprises, airlines, railways, five-star hotels, and manufacturing factories procuring packaged goods directly from manufacturers argued that individual retail labelling (such as MRP, standard unit sale price, retail helpline) was completely superfluous for bulk industrial use and institutional catering.
2. **Statutory Criteria Established:**
   - **Industrial Consumer (Rule 2(bb)):** Must satisfy two strict conditions:
     1. Buys packaged commodities *directly from the manufacturer*; AND
     2. For use *by that industry* (as raw materials, intermediate inputs, or internal operational use).
   - **Institutional Consumer (Rule 2(bc)):** Service institutions (transport services like Indian Railways / airlines, hotels, hospitals) that:
     1. Avail services or hire facilities; AND
     2. Buy packaged commodities *directly from the manufacturer for use by that institution*.
   - **Direct Consequence:** Where goods are sold directly to industrial or institutional consumers with appropriate packaging markings, they are exempt from standard retail pack sizes and retail MRP labelling, preventing frivolous enforcement seizures against bulk business transactions.

---

### B. Mandatory Weighing Equipment for LPG Cylinders (Rule 18(8))
1. **Consumer Protection Landmark:**
   - Under-filling of domestic and commercial LPG cylinders had been a perennial consumer grievance across Indian states. Consumers had no mechanism to verify whether a 14.2 kg domestic LPG cylinder or a 19 kg commercial cylinder contained the statutory net gas quantity upon delivery at their doorsteps.
2. **Statutory Specifications & Equipment Standards:**
   - **Target Entities:** Every marketing company (IOCL, BPCL, HPCL, etc.), manufacturer, packer, importer, or distributor.
   - **Instrument Class:** Must be an **Accuracy Class-III** weighing instrument (either digital or analogue check-weigher / non-automatic weighing instrument).
   - **Capacity & Verification Scale Interval:** Maximum capacity of **50 kg**, with verification scale interval **e = 10 g**.
   - **Delivery Man Mandate:** Under Rule 18(8)(2), distributors are legally obligated to equip **every delivery man** with this certified weighing instrument, enabling consumers to demand on-the-spot verification before taking delivery. Failure to provide weighing equipment constitutes a direct violation of Rule 18(8).

---

### C. Standardized Compounding Schedule (Rule 32(3) Table)
Prior to G.S.R. 359(E), compounding fees under Section 48 of the Act were discretionary, leading to wide disparities and arbitrary compounding sums imposed across different States. G.S.R. 359(E) inserted a rigid statutory table establishing differential fee liabilities between grassroots retail distributors and corporate manufacturers/importers:

| Statutory Section / Offence | Nature of Violation | Compounding Fee for Retailers / Wholesale Dealers | Compounding Fee for Manufacturers / Importers |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Section 29** | Penalty for use of unverified weight or measure | ₹2,000 | ₹10,000 |
| **Section 36(1)** | Penalty for manufacturing, packing, importing, selling non-conforming pre-packaged commodities (missing declarations, non-standard sizes) | ₹5,000 | ₹25,000 |
| **Section 36(2)** | Penalty for manufacture/sale of packages containing short net quantity | ₹10,000 | ₹50,000 |
| **Rule 18(2) / Section 36** | Selling products for more than the Maximum Retail Price (Dual pricing / Overcharging) | ₹2,000 | ₹5,000 |

---

### D. Second Schedule Standard Pack Size Modernization

G.S.R. 359(E) introduced extensive new permissible net quantity sizes into the Second Schedule to accommodate consumer convenience and modern SKU formats:

1. **Baby Food (Sr. No. 2):** Added intermediate trial and portion packs: **75g, 125g, 150g, 250g** (in addition to 500g, 1kg, 5kg, 10kg).
2. **Weaning Food (Sr. No. 3):** Added **350g, 400g**.
3. **Tea (Sr. No. 7):** Added popular family pack size: **750g**.
4. **Coffee (Sr. No. 8):** Fully restructured:
   - Below 25g: **No restriction** (allowing single-serve sachets of 1g, 2g, 5g, etc.).
   - Standard sizes: 25g, 50g, 75g, 100g, 125g, 150g, 200g, 250g, 500g, 750g, 1kg, 1.5kg, 2kg, and thereafter in multiples of 1kg.
5. **Beverages (Sr. No. 9):** Added **125g**.
6. **Edible Oils, Vanaspati, Ghee, Butter Oil (Sr. No. 10):**
   - By Weight: Below 50g no restriction; added **175g, 300g, 750g**.
   - By Volume: Below 50 ml no restriction; added **175 ml, 300 ml, 750 ml**.
7. **Aerated Soft Drinks (Sr. No. 12):** Added large party sizes: **2.5kg, 3kg**.
8. **Toothpaste (Sr. No. 16):** Omitted the archaic restriction **"(in can only)"**, thereby making standard packaging rules uniformly applicable to modern laminated plastic/aluminium squeeze tubes.

---

## 4. Comprehensive Q&A Reference for SIH Problem Statement 26035

### Q1: What are the statutory definitions of "Industrial Consumer" and "Institutional Consumer" under G.S.R. 359(E)?
**Answer:**
- **Industrial Consumer (Rule 2(bb)):** A consumer who buys packaged commodities directly from the manufacturer for use by that industry.
- **Institutional Consumer (Rule 2(bc)):** Any institution which hires or avails of facilities or services in connection with transport, hotels, hospitals, or similar service institutions that buy packaged commodities directly from the manufacturer for use by that institution.

### Q2: What mandatory equipment must be provided for checking the quantity of LPG cylinders under Rule 18(8)?
**Answer:**
Under Rule 18(8), all LPG marketing companies, manufacturers, packers, importers, or distributors must:
1. Maintain a digital or analogue check-weigher or non-automatic weighing instrument of **Accuracy Class-III**, having a maximum capacity of **50 kg** and verification scale interval **e = 10 g**.
2. Provide this weighing instrument to every **delivery man** so that consumers can measure and verify the correct weight of the LPG cylinder at the time of delivery.

### Q3: When did the LPG weighing requirement under Rule 18(8) come into force?
**Answer:** While G.S.R. 359(E) generally came into force on its date of gazette publication (6th June, 2013), Rule 18(8) came into force with effect from **1st July, 2013**.

### Q4: What is the statutory compounding fee under Rule 32(3) for short net quantity violations under Section 36(2)?
**Answer:**
- For retailers or wholesale dealers: **₹10,000**.
- For manufacturers or importers: **₹50,000**.

### Q5: What is the compounding amount under Rule 32(3) for selling packaged goods above the Maximum Retail Price (MRP)?
**Answer:**
- For retailers or wholesale dealers: **₹2,000**.
- For manufacturers or importers: **₹5,000**.

### Q6: What significant change was made to the coffee packaging standards under the Second Schedule by G.S.R. 359(E)?
**Answer:** G.S.R. 359(E) substituted the entire entry for Serial No. 8 (Coffee) to permit complete freedom below 25g ("Below 25g no restriction"), enabling single-use sachets, and established standard increments of 25g, 50g, 75g, 100g, 125g, 150g, 200g, 250g, 500g, 750g, 1kg, 1.5kg, 2kg, and multiples of 1kg.

### Q7: Who was the signatory to G.S.R. 359(E), and what was the official file reference?
**Answer:** G.S.R. 359(E) was signed by **Manoj Kumar Parida**, Joint Secretary to the Government of India, Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution, under file reference **`F. No. WM-10(6)/2013`**.

- **Document Title:** The Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Amendment) Rules, 2014 / विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) संशोधन नियम, 2014

- **Gazette Notification Number:** G.S.R. 137 [सा.का.नि. 137]
- **Gazette Volume & Number:** Extract from The Gazette of India: Part II—Section 3—Sub-section (i), appearing on Page Nos. 730—731, published by Authority, dated 21-06-2014
- **Date of Notification:** 16th June, 2014
- **Date of Enforcement:** 1st July, 2014 ("They shall come into force with effect from 1st July, 2014" / "ये 1 जुलाई, 2014 को प्रवृत्त होंगे।")
- **Administrative Ministry:** Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Consumer Affairs) / उपभोक्ता मामले, खाद्य और सार्वजनिक वितरण मंत्रालय (उपभोक्ता मामले विभाग)
- **Official File Reference:** `F. No. WM-10(38)/2013` [फा. सं. डब्ल्यू एम-10(38)/2013]

## 2. Complete Verbatim Statutory Text (Bilingual)

### Part A: Official English Gazette Text

```text
EXTRACT FROM THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : PART II, SEC. 3, SUB-SEC. (i)
Appearing on Page Nos. 730—731
Dated: 21-06-2014

MINISTRY OF CONSUMER AFFAIRS, FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION
(Department of Consumer Affairs)
NOTIFICATION
New Delhi, the 16th June, 2014

G.S.R. 137.—In exercise of powers conferred by sub-section (1) read with clause (j) of sub-section (2) of Section 52 of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009 (1 of 2010), the Central Government hereby makes the following rules further to amend the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules 2011, namely :—

1. (1) These rules may be called as Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) (Amendment) Rules, 2014.
   (2) They shall come into force with effect from 1st July, 2014.

2. In the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules 2011, in rule 6, after sub-rule (7), the following shall be inserted, namely :—

   "(8) Every package containing soap, shampoos, tooth pastes and other cosmetics and toiletries shall bear at the top of its principal display panel a red or as the case may be, brown dot for products of non-vegetarian origin and a green dot products of vegetarian origin.".

[F. No. WM-10(38)/2013]
B. N. DIXIT, Director (Legal Metrology)

Note: The principal rules were published in the Gazette of India, vide notification number G.S.R. 202(E), dated the 7th March, 2011 and last amended vide notification number G.S.R. 359(E), dated the 6th June, 2013.

MGIPRRND—2551 CAF&PD/2014—100 Copies.
```

```text
सा.का.नि. 137.—केन्द्रीय सरकार, विधिक माप विज्ञान अधिनियम, 2009 (2010 का 1) की धारा 52 की उप-धारा (2) के खण्ड (ज) के साथ पठित उप-धारा (1) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) नियम, 2011 का और संशोधन करने के लिए निम्नलिखित नियम बनाती है, अर्थात् :—

1. (1) इन नियमों का संक्षिप्त नाम विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) संशोधन नियम, 2014 है।
   (2) ये 1 जुलाई, 2014 को प्रवृत्त होंगे।

2. विधिक माप विज्ञान (पैकेज में रखी हुई वस्तुएं) नियम, 2011 के, नियम 6 के उप-नियम (7) के पश्चात् निम्नलिखित अंतःस्थापित किया जाएगा, अर्थात् :—

   "(8) साबुन, शैम्पू, दंत मंजन तथा अन्य श्रृंगार एवं प्रसाधन सामग्रियों के प्रत्येक पैकेज पर उसके मुख्य प्रदर्शन पैनल के सबसे ऊपर मांसाहारी उद्भव के उत्पादों के लिए लाल या भूरी यथास्थिति बिंदी और शाकाहारी उद्भव के उत्पादों के लिए हरी बिंदी होगी।"

[फा. सं. डब्ल्यू एम-10(38)/2013]
बी. एन. दीक्षित, निदेशक (विधिक माप विज्ञान)
```

## 3. Structural Legal Analysis & Cross-Rule Harmonization

### 1. Introduction of Vegetarian / Non-Vegetarian Origin Indicators in Cosmetics (Rule 6(8))
- **Target Category:** Every package containing **soap, shampoos, tooth pastes and other cosmetics and toiletries**.
- **Display Location:** Mandatorily placed at the **top of its principal display panel (PDP)**.
- **Color Coding Regime:**
  - **Non-Vegetarian Origin:** A **Red dot** or **Brown dot** (*"लाल या भूरी यथास्थिति बिंदी"*).
  - **Vegetarian Origin:** A **Green dot** (*"हरी बिंदी"*).
- **Statutory Purpose:** Following representations regarding religious and personal sentiments of consumers purchasing personal care, oral care, and toiletry products containing animal-derived fats/tallow (such as sodium tallowate in soaps, gelatin, animal glycerin, or bone phosphate in toothpastes), this notification created an affirmative consumer disclosure obligation mirroring the food packaging standards under the Food Safety and Standards (Packaging and Labelling) Regulations, 2011.
- **Subsequent Legal Evolution:** This rule was subject to significant litigation and industry review regarding testing methodology, leading to subsequent refinements and harmonized circulars under the Legal Metrology regime.

---

## 4. Practical Impact Matrix & Verification Checklist

| Compliance Field | Statutory Requirement | Statutory Citation |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Applicable Goods** | Soaps, Shampoos, Toothpastes, Cosmetics, Toiletries | Rule 6(8) |
| **Mandatory Panel Location** | Top of Principal Display Panel (PDP) | Rule 6(8) |
| **Non-Veg Symbol** | Red or Brown circular dot | Rule 6(8) |
| **Veg Symbol** | Green circular dot | Rule 6(8) |
| **Effective Date** | 1st July, 2014 | Rule 1(2) |
| **Penal Provision** | Section 36(1) of the Act (Non-standard package declarations) | Section 36(1) read with Rule 32 |
